reading_f177.md

# fr. 15571 f. 177r-v, Forget to Villeroy, 31 Dec 1585: the reading

fr. 15571 f. 177, Gallica canvas 185 right (recto, 29 cipher lines) and canvas 186 left (verso, 11 cipher lines,
then clear text). The archivist's heading: *Lettre de Mr Forget a Mr de Villeroy sur les desseins d'assieger Pons ou
Taillebourg, mais le Vicomte de Turenne ayant paru en campagne avec gens et canons, on a changé d'advis*.

What was done, 22 Oct 2026: Michael Brackx's transcription was decoded with the merged key and read sentence by
sentence against each figure's letter set. Every break was cropped from the full-resolution image. Twelve labels were
changed (one added, one code merged); the leaf key adds seven values. **A contemporary clear copy of this cipher was
found on the preceding leaf**, f. 176r (canvas 184 right, from the marginal note *Mons. du Mayne* to the foot) and
f. 176v (canvas 185 left, signed *Mr Forget*). The copy was read from the image and used to choose between the
letters a figure allows and to check the reading; it supplies no word that the figures do not carry. Where the figures
and the copy disagree, the reading follows the figures and marks the word.

Marks: a word in square brackets with a question mark is carried only in part by its figures; `[…]` is a figure that
gives no letter of the word; `[cs]` is a figure left unread. The barred double g (`gg`), the `me` sign and one barred
infinity sign are nulls on this leaf (the copy has nothing in their place, at seven places) and are left out of the
text. Code 73 reads *le Roy* and code 64 *quelque*, both from the copy. Accents and punctuation are modern; the
spelling is what the figures give.

## The clear opening (not re-read in full)

Four lines in clear above the cipher, in Forget's hand, partly legible: *Depuis Mr d'Aumont … Limozin …* and
*… persuader avec de nouvelles raisons de tenter …*. They are not part of this reading.

## The cipher

[l. 1] … assiegeant Pons et Tailebourg, que l'on [l. 2] disoit que l'on pouvoit faire tout ensemb[l. 3]le, et que
indubitablement l'on les empo[l. 4]rteroit; et que ce eust esté aux ennemis, [l. 5] specialement pour Tailebourg,
oster un [l. 6] grand revenu qu'ils tirent du passaige [l. 7] de la Charante, où ils levent de [l. 8] grandes
impositions, et remettre le Roy en la [l. 9] jouissance des tailes de tout ce pais, [l. 10] que l'on dict monter
beaucoup.

Que oultre cela le pais [l. 11] nous offroit trant mil escus pour aider [l. 12] à la despence du siege.

Et y estans [l. 13] desja si disposés, que nous consentions que, [l. 14] s'il venoit advis de quelque [?remuement] en
Guie[l. 15]ne, que Monsieur le mareschal de Matignon [l. 16] s'y en iroit avec quelque peu de [?forces] s'y
[l. 17] [?oposer], et preparer aussi ce qui seroit [l. 18] necssaire [?pour] y recevoir ceste armée [l. 19] à la
primevere; cependant que nous conti[l. 20]nurions [?ce siège].

Mais comme nous estions pour [l. 21] clore ce pacquet, le dict sieur de Ma[l. 22]tignon a eu advis de Bourdeaux que
le vicomte de Turenne [cs] [l. 23] estoit paru en campaigne avec forces [l. 24] et canons.

Cela nous a soudainement faict [l. 25] changer nostre premier advis, parce qu'il ne veult [l. 26] point partir qu'il
n'emene avec lui [?la moitié] de [l. 27] l'armée; de sorte que nostre dicte depesche a [l. 28] esté convertie en celle
que vous avés icy.

Voil[l. 29]a comment noz opinions sont erantes.

Je [v. l. 1] pensois trouver icy le dict sieur de [v. l. 2] [?Matignon] tout resolu de ce que [?nous] devions
[v. l. 3] faire.

Mais je vous [?confesse] que je l'en [v. l. 4] ay veu aussi incertain que les autres, comme à [v. l. 5] la verité il
y a prou de raisons de part [v. l. 6] et d'autre jusques icy.

Nous sommes [v. l. 7] tous resolus à la Gascongne, et croy que [v. l. 8] la responce de nostre depesche s'y
trouver[v. l. 9]a […] conforme.

Mais toutesfois crains, pour le che[v. l. 10]min que Monsieur du Mayne doibt tenir, que nous n'y employons
[v. l. 11] beaucoup plus de tems que nous ne [?desirons].

## What stays open

- [?pais] (r. l. 9): the first figure is read as p after correction; confirmed by the clear copy f. 176r, which has *ce pays* (its next instance, l. 10, is *le pays*).
- [?remuement] (r. l. 14): *r e m u e d e n t*: the infinity figure (d) stands where m is needed. Copy: *remuement*.
- [?forces] (r. l. 16): the figure 6 (i/n/s) stands for e.
- [?oposer] (r. l. 16-17): the large C at the end of l. 16 (Cl = l) stands for p.
- [?pour] (r. l. 18): *p o u* then a small c (p/l/s) for r.
- [?ce siège] (r. l. 20): *c e s i e* then U and 6, which give no *g e*. Copy: *ce siege* (f. 176r), which agrees with the figures' *ce*.
- [?la moitié] (r. l. 26): *l a m [o] t i e*, one figure short (no o). Copy: *la moictié*.
- [cs] (r. l. 22): one figure after code 84, not read (the copy has *estoit paru*).
- [?Matignon] (v. l. 2): the third vowel is the figure 7 (o): *Matognon*, a writer's slip.
- [?nous] (v. l. 2): the code is 26 (*vous*) on the image; the copy has *nous*, and only *nous* fits *devions*.
- [?confesse] (v. l. 3): *c o n f* + two figures (ao, B) + *ss e*: one figure too many.
- je l'[…] ay veu (v. l. 3-4): after *l* the figures give *e n*; the clear copy has *l'en(?)*, which the figures decide: *je l'en ay veu*.
- trouvera […] conforme (v. l. 9): one figure U between the two words, no letter fits.
- [?desirons] (v. l. 11): *d e s o n s*, two letters missing at the end of the cipher, before the clear text.

Files: corrections `brackx2026/corrections/f177.tsv`, corrected figures `brackx2026/corrections/f177_cipher.txt`, leaf
key `key_cipher1_f177.json`, passes `texts/reading_f177_log.md`, translation `texts/reading_f177_en.md`.

## Checked against the clear copy (22 Oct 2026)

The contemporary decipherment on fr. 15571 f. 176r-v (transcribed in `brackx2026/copies/f176_f177.txt`; full
comparison in `reading_f177_vs_copy.md`) runs the whole cipher. It confirms 73 = *le Roy* and 64 = *quelque* here,
the nulls, *ce pais* and *je l'en ay veu*. It gives a word for every remaining [?…]; the figures carry most of them
only in part (one or two figures short or misread), so they stay marked here, and the copy is the reading for them.
The cipher has *dicte* in "nostre dicte depesche" (r. l. 27) where the copy omits it.
Reading

f176_f177.txt

# fr. 15571 f. 176r (lower part) and f. 176v: the contemporary decipherment of the cipher on f. 177r-v

Forget to Villeroy, 31 Dec 1585. Ark `btv1b90618802`: f. 176r = canvas 184 right, ll. 1-11 below the marginal
note *Mons. du Mayne* (the twenty lines above it are another passage and are not transcribed here); f. 176v =
canvas 185 left, ll. 12-22 and the signature. Read from the full-size openapi.bnf.fr IIIF image, 22 Oct 2026.
- Line breaks are the manuscript's; the line numbers are added. Spelling is the copy's; abbreviations are
  expanded in square brackets (p[ou]r, n[ost]re, M[onsieu]r, S[ieu]r, led[it]); accents are not added except é
  in past participles; punctuation is sparse in the copy and is kept sparse.
- `(?)` marks a doubtful word; `[…]` an unread word. There are no interlinear additions on these lines.
- Underlinings: the copyist (or a later reader) underlined names and some words (*Pons*, *Taillebourg*,
  *Charante*, *trante mil*, *Matignon*, *Turenne*, *Bourdeaux*, *Gascongne*, *Mr du Mayne*); not marked below.

[f. 176r, margin, left of l. 1:] Mons. du Mayne
1. assiegeant Pons et Taillebourg que l'on disoit que l'on pouvoit f[air]e tout ensemble
2. et que Indubitablem[en]t on(?) les emporteroit et que ce eust esté aux ennemys specialleme[n]t
3. p[ou]r Taillebourg oster ung grand revenu qu'ilz tirent du passage de la Charante
4. ou ilz levent de grandes Impositions et remettre le Roy en la Jouissance des tailles
5. de tout ce pays que l'on dict monter beaucoup que oultre cela le pays nous offroit
6. trante mil escuz p[ou]r ayder a la desp[en]ce(?) du siege et y estans desja si disposez
7. que nous consentions que s'il venoit advis de quelque remuement en Guyenne(?) que
8. M[onsieu]r le Mar[esch]al de Matignon s'y en Iroit avec quelque peu de forces s'y
9. opposer et preparer aussi ce qui seroit necess[ai]re p[ou]r y recevoir ceste armée a la
10. primevere ce pendant que nous continuerions ce siege Mais comme nous
11. estions p[ou]r clorre ce pacquet led[it] S[ieu]r de Matignon a eu advis de Bourdeaux
[f. 176v]
12. que le Viconte de Turenne estoit paru en Campagne avec forces et Canons
13. Cela nous a soudain[ement](?) faict changer n[ost]re premier advis parce qu'il ne veult
14. point partir qu'il n'emmeine avec luy la moictie de l'armée de sorte que
15. n[ost]re depesche a esté convertie en celle que vous avez Icy voila comment
16. noz opinions sont errantes Je pensois trouver Icy led[it] S[ieu]r de Matignon
17. tout resolu de ce que nous debvions f[air]e Mais je vous confesse que je l'en(?)
18. ay veu aussi incertain que les autres comme a la verité Il y a prou de
19. raisons de part et d'autre jusques Icy Nous sommes tous resoluz a la Gascongne
20. et croy que la responce de n[ost]re depesche s'y trouvera conforme Mais toutesfois
21. crains p[ou]r le chemin que M[onsieu]r du Mayne doibt tenir que nous n'y employons
22. beaucoup plus de temps que nous ne desirons ./.
M[onsieu]r Forget ./.

Notes on doubtful places
- l. 2 *on(?)*: a short word of two letters with a cross-stroke after *Indubitablement*; *l'on* (as in l. 1)
  is possible if the l is merged with the preceding flourish.
- l. 6 *desp[en]ce(?)*: written *desp* + a superscript; *despence* or *despense*.
- l. 7 *Guyenne(?)*: *Guy* then an ending written over or struck through; the sense is certain.
- l. 13 *soudain[ement](?)*: *soudain* with a final flourish, the same stroke that ends *Indubitablem[en]t* in
  l. 2; read as the -ment abbreviation, but plain *soudain* is possible.
- l. 17 *l'en(?)*: *l* + the form this hand uses for *en* (as in *en la Jouissance*, l. 4, and *s'y en Iroit*,
  l. 8); *luy* is the other possible reading.
- l. 18 *autres*: the *au* is enclosed in a loop, perhaps a correction.
- The right margin of f. 176r shows ink at the line ends of ll. 9-11 that belongs to the facing leaf, not to
  this text.
Translation

reading_f177_en.md

# fr. 15571 f. 177r-v, Forget to Villeroy, 31 Dec 1585: English translation

Translated from the reading (`reading_f177.md`), with the same holes. A word in square brackets with a question mark
translates a word the figures carry only in part; `[…]` is a figure that gives no letter; `[cs]` a figure left
unread. Line numbers are those of the cipher (r. = recto, v. = verso). The nulls are left out, as in the French.

## The cipher

[l. 1] … besieging Pons and Taillebourg, which it was [l. 2] said could be done both together, and that [l. 3]
beyond doubt we would carry [l. 4] them; and that this would have been for the enemy, [l. 5] especially as regards
Taillebourg, the loss of a [l. 6] great revenue that they draw from the crossing [l. 7] of the Charente, where they
levy [l. 8] heavy dues, and would restore the King to the [l. 9] enjoyment of the taille of all this [?country],
[l. 10] which is said to come to a great deal.

That besides this the country [l. 11] offered us thirty thousand écus to help [l. 12] with the cost of the siege.

And being [l. 13] already so disposed that we agreed that, [l. 14] if news came of some [?stirring] in
Guyenne, [l. 15] Monsieur the Marshal de Matignon [l. 16] would go there with some few [?forces] to [l. 17]
[?oppose] it, and also prepare what would be [l. 18] necessary [?to] receive this army there [l. 19] in the spring;
meanwhile we would [l. 20] continue [?this siege].

But as we were about to [l. 21] close this packet, the said Sieur de [l. 22] Matignon had news from Bordeaux that
the Vicomte de Turenne [cs] [l. 23] had taken the field with forces [l. 24] and guns.

That has suddenly made us [l. 25] change our first plan, because he will not [l. 26] leave without taking with him
[?half] of [l. 27] the army; so that our said despatch has [l. 28] been turned into the one you have here.

There you [l. 29] see how our opinions wander.

I [v. l. 1] thought to find the said Sieur de [v. l. 2] [?Matignon] here quite resolved on what [?we] should [v. l. 3]
do.

But I [?confess] to you that I […] [v. l. 4] have found him as uncertain as the others, since in [v. l. 5] truth there
are plenty of reasons on both sides [v. l. 6] so far.

We are [v. l. 7] all resolved on Gascony, and I believe that [v. l. 8] the answer to our despatch will be found [v. l. 9]
[…] in agreement with it.

But all the same I fear, for the road [v. l. 10] that Monsieur du Mayne must take, that we shall spend [v. l. 11]
much more time on it than we [?wish].

f. 20r read in part

Mayenne to the King, January 1586 (endorsed 'Mr du Mayne du vjme Januier 1586'), the end of the f. 18 despatch: the remains of four regiments and Colonel Heid's Swiss to meet again on the 6th of next month; he will not be drawn into the Limousin and Quercy, since intercepted letters of Turenne to the King of Navarre show Tulle about to compound for four or five thousand ecus and Lamorez to withdraw to Navarre.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (86% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

f19v_copy.md

# fr. 15572 f. 19v (end): the contemporary clear copy of f. 20r

> Transcribed by Michael Brackx from the full-resolution Gallica image (canvas 22 left), issue #87, reply of 23 Oct 2026.
> As written: the writer's spelling and capitals, no added accents or apostrophes, abbreviations expanded in brackets,
> `[?word]` doubtful, `[?]` one unread word. This copy is the reading of the cipher leaf f. 20r.

Les restes des regim[en]s de [?Sacremore] de [?Vic] de [?] [?]
& de la Roche [?Montoisy] Et celuy des Suisses du Colonel Heid Nous
auons prins jo[u]r de nous retrouuer ensemble dans le [?vj]me du prochain
auec Resolution de ne nous arrester a aucune entreprinse si elle nest [?tres]
facile q[ue] [?ce] fust honte de la negligence Plus[ieu]rs de ceulz qui sont de deca
me voudroient bien attirer au Lymosin & au Quercy Mais je voy q[ue] cela
me feroit consommer trop de temps Ayant aussi bien appris par quelques
lettres qui ont este Interceptees du Viconte de Turenne au Roy de Nau[ar]re
quilz sont sur les termes de composer po[u]r f[air]e sortir la garnison qui est
dans Tulle moyennant quatre ou cinq mil escuz que demandent ceulz
de lad[ic]te ville Ce quilz effectueront dautant plustost quilz me scauront

M[onsieu]r du Mayne du [?vj]me Januier

# fr. 15572 f. 19v (end): the contemporary clear copy of f. 20r

> Transcribed by Michael Brackx from the full-resolution Gallica image (canvas 22 left), issue #87, reply of 23 Oct 2026.
> As written: the writer's spelling and capitals, no added accents or apostrophes, abbreviations expanded in brackets,
> `[?word]` doubtful, `[?]` one unread word. This copy is the reading of the cipher leaf f. 20r.

Les restes des regim[en]s de [?Sacremore] de [?Vic] de [?] [?]
& de la Roche [?Montoisy] Et celuy des Suisses du Colonel Heid Nous
auons prins jo[u]r de nous retrouuer ensemble dans le [?vj]me du prochain
auec Resolution de ne nous arrester a aucune entreprinse si elle nest [?tres]
facile q[ue] [?ce] fust honte de la negligence Plus[ieu]rs de ceulz qui sont de deca
me voudroient bien attirer au Lymosin & au Quercy Mais je voy q[ue] cela
me feroit consommer trop de temps Ayant aussi bien appris par quelques
lettres qui ont este Interceptees du Viconte de Turenne au Roy de Nau[ar]re
quilz sont sur les termes de composer po[u]r f[air]e sortir la garnison qui est
dans Tulle moyennant quatre ou cinq mil escuz que demandent ceulz
de lad[ic]te ville Ce quilz effectueront dautant plustost quilz me scauront

reading_f20r_en.md

# f. 20r, English (from the clear copy at the end of f. 19v)

> Translation by Michael Brackx (issue #87, 23 Oct 2026), with the same holes as the copy.

The remains of the regiments of [?Sacremore], of [?Vic], of [?] [?] and of la Roche [?Montoisy], and that of the
Swiss of Colonel Heid. We have fixed a day to meet again on the [?6th] of next month, resolved not to stop at any
enterprise unless it is [?very] easy, since [?it] would be a shame for the negligence. Several of those on this side
would like to draw me into the Limousin and the Quercy, but I see that this would make me spend too much time, having
also learnt from some intercepted letters of the vicomte de Turenne to the King of Navarre that they are about to come
to terms to get the garrison out of Tulle, for four or five thousand écus that the people of the said town ask; which
they will do all the sooner when they know I am approaching, so that I hold it certain that it will be abandoned, and
that Lamorez, who is inside, will withdraw to the said King of Navarre.

Endorsed: Monsieur du Mayne, the [?6th] of January 1586.

Key: key_cipher1.json, brackx2026

  • Bibliothèque nationale de France, fr. 15572 (Gallica btv1b9061879d; canvases above, copies ff. 155–157 on canvases 162–164 (the end of the f. 143 copy at the head of f. 155v, canvas 163), f. 196v on canvas 204, cribs f. 14v/15r on canvases 16–17 and ff. 18–19, f. 19r on canvas 21) and fr. 15571 f. 177 (btv1b90618802, canvases 185–186; its clear copy f. 176r–v on canvases 184–185). Each canvas is a double page.
  • Satoshi Tomokiyo, cryptiana, Henry III’s Cipher with Ambassadors: the Cipher-1 tables and the openings of ff. 143, 150 and 154.
  • Michael Brackx, issues 64, 68–71, 73, 77–79 and 83 (October 2026): transcriptions, per-leaf keys, code values, the clear copies.
  • Other ciphers in these papers, each with its own page: Mayenne–Forget’s Cipher-2, Matignon’s Cipher-3, and Saint-Luc’s letter (fr. 15572 f. 43).
